## Local practice release1192609 — verification detail (2026-10-22)

Client implementation commit8cd6db9 adds the real local practice flow and engine tests. Delivery commit75e874a packages1192609 while preserving the completed1192608 account stages. Server changes in this cycle are documentation only.

The first practice candidate exposed a real stock unit serialization issue: an empty advances_to list becomes an empty saved string, so reconstructing the leader restores its normal type advancement branches. The final generator adds a forever remove_advancement modification covering the24 supported types, alongside the explicit disabled AMLA. A retained pre-fix exporter fails the actual Spearman reconstruction assertion; the final generator passes two construction/serialization cycles for every tested leader. The initial APK/native candidate and its journal remain under build/audit-team-practice-delivery-20261022/candidate-before-persistent-leader-lock. Both paths assert exact saved leaders/controllers, two reconstruction cycles and a complete AI round. The human turn is ended by a test-only event; these tests do not click the GUI. Test-only no-audio execution excludes the audio log domain because the unchanged stock playlist refers to absent undead_siege.ogg; raw failed attempts are retained and all other logged errors remain strict.

The isolated host engine uses GCC16.2/Boost1.91 and SDL3.4.2/image3.4.0/mixer3.2.0; host Pango1.56.3/ICU76.1/curl8.14.1/OpenSSL3.5.7 differ from the existing Android prerequisites. At the initial audit, C/C++ compilers, CMake, Ninja, Java, SCons, ADB and MongoDB binaries were absent from PATH. The copied Windows `.venv` is not a working Linux build environment. The report jumped from Git results to the final summary without Java, source, MSYS2 package, Android, emulator or MongoDB results. The user subsequently confirmed that they pressed Ctrl+C or stopped it. Its zero BLOCKED/UNKNOWN counts do **not** establish a complete inventory. Those remaining versions and source integrity still await an uninterrupted report. Do not upgrade or install a proposed toolchain based on this partial run.

Archived MSYS2 package metadata lists MINGW64/UCRT64 GCC 16.2.0-3, MINGW64 Boost 1.91.0-3, MSYS GCC 15.3.0-1, MSYS Python 3.12.13-1, UCRT64 Python 3.14.7-1, Git 2.55.0-1, OpenSSL 3.6.3-1, make 4.4.1-3, Ninja 1.13.2-1, Meson 1.12.0-1 and MSYS runtime 3.6.10-3. These are archive labels, not verified active tool versions. SCons/CMake/JDK/Android tools were not found in that archive inventory and may be installed separately.

The surviving custom-client requirement is only `scons>=4.5.0`. Copied environment directory names suggest SCons 4.11.0, and dependency archive filenames suggest Boost 1.88.0 and SDL3 3.4.2; neither proves a working installation. The differing Boost versions need investigation.

## Upstream reference versus intended behavior

[Upstream 1.19.26 Android Gradle configuration](https://github.com/wesnoth/wesnoth/blob/1.19.26/packaging/android/app/build.gradle) declares compile/target SDK 35, minimum SDK 23 and NDK 26.3.11579264. The tag's other build files declare AGP 8.7.0 and Gradle 8.11.1. Its native `build-sources` script defaults to NDK 23.1.7779620 and calls a Linux-specific `llvm-strip` path. These are **reference values**, not confirmed requirements of the missing custom Windows build.

The populated reference Android code imports ZIP game data into its external-files `gamedata` directory and uses `manual_install=true` to bypass downloading. No OBB loader was found in that Android code. The intended OBB workflow requires recovering or implementing the custom loader and determining the package ID, versionCode and asset layout. Merely renaming an archive to `.obb` does not prove integration.

The stock client/server use Wesnoth's native TCP protocol, normally port 15000, with a four-byte handshake and length-prefixed compressed WML. A replacement HTTP/JSON server would not automatically work with this client.

[Upstream 1.19.26 database code](https://github.com/wesnoth/wesnoth/blob/1.19.26/src/server/common/dbconn.hpp) uses optional MariaDB/MySQL integration. MongoDB is the user's intended database, but no surviving custom code implements it or reads `.env`. The existing `.env` has key `DB` containing an SRV-style MongoDB URI with a non-loopback destination; values are intentionally omitted. That configuration does not establish a local offline database. Decide the local MongoDB configuration after confirming the Windows installation. Stock guest LAN play without a database can be an earlier checkpoint; MongoDB persistence remains a separate required integration.

## Repository handling

Initial Git remote URLs contain an embedded access token. Never print raw remotes; rotate the exposed credential and use secure Git authentication. This audit adds `.gitignore` rules to prevent accidental inclusion of `.env`, credentials, reports and generated outputs. It does not publish the empty source trees. Existing source files remain untouched for recovery/comparison.

## First Windows report correction

The first real Windows PowerShell 5.1 run reached both Git version probes and saved a partial report. The user confirmed that they pressed Ctrl+C or stopped the run. This explains the early exit; it is not evidence of a Java or Windows installation failure. Controlled Linux PowerShell tests reproduced the reporting flaw: pipeline cancellation can run `finally`, skip `catch`, save a report, and exit zero in the original collector.

COMPLETE describes inventory coverage only; missing tools or empty source can still be reported as BLOCKED.

In the installed Git for Windows version, this error comes from [finalizing a temporary object into its hash filename](https://github.com/git-for-windows/git/blob/v2.53.0.windows.2/object-file.c#L626-L689). This is the documented [Windows sharing-violation message](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/debug/system-error-codes--0-499-). No cleanup failure was reported.
- Server object `55f35e7...` exists as a 183-byte ReadOnly/Archive file. Server object `3c5b8ba...` and client object `1a38d17...` were absent. This metadata does not verify object integrity and does not warrant deleting objects or clearing attributes.

Do not repeatedly reboot, blindly kill processes or disable antivirus.

## Process Monitor finding

The user supplied workspace-root `git-objects.CSV`: UTF-8 BOM, 604 events, SHA256 `ffd0435cf7528a8b098cdcfdc697009a3da8c986a169bdf1195e472d22ee67bf`. The raw trace remains a local diagnostic and is not committed. CSV line references below count the header as line 1.

The direct conflicting handle owner is **svchost.exe, PID 15800**. Two sequences show it opening a newly created Git temporary file with **ShareMode: Read, Write**, omitting Delete. Windows requires compatible [delete sharing for rename/delete access](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/fileapi/nf-fileapi-createfilea).

| CSV line | Time in capture | Event |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 228 | 08:26:50.6595596 | svchost 15800 opens `25\tmp_obj_t7Q84k` without delete sharing. |
| 234, 236 | 08:26:50.6632212 / .6640069 | Git 25144 closes its own write handle, then requests Delete access and receives SHARING VIOLATION. |
| 257, 266, 270 | 08:26:50.6659062 onward | svchost closes the handle; Git obtains Delete access and deletes its failed temporary object. This is cleanup, not a successful fetch. |
| 554, 562 | 08:27:12.3194636 / .3220562 | Same conflict on `f6\tmp_obj_pfWOR9`, with Git 5804. |
| 591, 600, 604 | 08:27:12.3259551 onward | svchost closes; Git can open for Delete and clean up. |
| 530, 534, 537 | 08:27:12.3069810 onward | In a successful comparison, svchost closes `c6\tmp_obj_iKJZwc` before Git requests Delete; Git renames it to the final `c6c5d903...` object. |

The conflicting handles last approximately 6.35 and 6.49 milliseconds, consistent with Resource Monitor missing them. The trace contains nine SHARING VIOLATION events and no ACCESS DENIED result. Git's displayed `Permission denied` therefore obscures the captured sharing conflict. Normal file-mapping statuses such as FILE LOCKED WITH ONLY READERS are not counted as these failures.

`MsMpEng.exe` PID 4176 also maps/queries the files, including in successful sequences, but this CSV does not establish it as the conflicting handle owner. Do not attribute the failure to Defender or add antivirus exclusions from this evidence. `svchost.exe` hosts services; its name alone does not identify the specific service. The next read-only command is recorded in `WORKFLOW.md`. PIDs can change or be reused after exit/restart, so a later PID lookup must correspond to the captured process. No service has been stopped and Windows recovery is not yet verified.

## First Windows build: source path discovery failure (2026-10-11)

The user ran Build-WindowsServer.ps1 and returned the output saved as `diagnostics/server-build-20261011-135703-585.txt`. The helper reported `dirname: command not found` at line 14, followed by `Source: /` and `Build: //build/windows-mingw64`. CMake then rejected source `D:/Test/msys64` because it contains no CMakeLists.txt. The build exited 1 and the PowerShell guard correctly prevented the protocol test. No successful Windows CMake configuration, C++ compilation or native protocol run is established.

Cause: PowerShell invokes Bash with --noprofile --norc. The inherited PATH did not locate dirname before the helper added /usr/bin. The failed dirname command was nested inside cd's argument: its empty output formed /.., cd succeeded at the MSYS root, and set -e did not prevent subsequent work using that wrong root. This is a helper startup defect, not evidence that CMake, Git, the restored source or MSYS2 needs reinstalling. The earlier Linux path fixtures included /usr/bin in their inherited PATH and missed this condition.

The fix establishes MSYSTEM/MINGW_PREFIX and the process PATH before resolving any paths, handles an empty/unset inherited PATH, converts the script path with the existing explicit cygpath tool and finds its directory using Bash parameter expansion instead of an external dirname command. The cd/pwd chain now has no nested external lookup and clears CDPATH for this operation. Before invoking native build tools, the helper requires nonempty root CMakeLists.txt, engine/UPSTREAM.json and engine/src/server/wesnothd/server.cpp. An incomplete or wrongly located checkout stops with its exact missing input rather than sending an unintended root to CMake. The next-step footer now correctly says protocol test before manual server run.

This report also confirms native CMake 4.3.0, UCRT Ninja 1.13.2, MINGW64 GCC 16.2.0-3, Boost 1.91.0-3, OpenSSL 3.6.3-1, ICU 78.3-4, zlib 1.3.2-2 and bzip2 1.0.8-4. Preserve these versions. No tool installation, environment-variable change outside the build process, build-directory deletion or service change is needed for this correction.

Validation: Bash syntax passed. The only blocked check was Meson/Python binding: the helper compared realpath(interpreter) and realpath(sys.executable) as strings and printed that they differed. It did not print either path. The compiler/archive check was explicitly skipped, the helper exited2, and scratch was retained at C:\Users\Uni\AppData\Local\Temp\wesnoth text toolchain eff1713e003544428c863f5bafeeb4aa. Do not record a successful Windows compile/archive bridge or full text build from this report.

The supplied msys64.rar lists usr/bin/python.exe, python3.exe and python3.12.exe as separate regular10,026-byte entries with identical CRC2DFFC322 and no link metadata. This supports copied aliases as the likely explanation, although the actual compared Windows pair remains unknown. [MSYS2 documents default symlinks as independent deep copies](https://www.msys2.org/docs/symlinks/); comparing path names or only [samefile](https://docs.python.org/3.12/library/os.path.html#os.path.samefile) is insufficient for such copies. The archive CRC is supporting evidence, not a substitute for checking the installed executable bytes.

The corrected helper prints the selected Python/platform/version, Meson launcher/module/version and shebang interpreter before comparison. It accepts samefile identity or byte-identical executable copies within the same directory, then invokes the shebang interpreter with isolated Python flags and a bounded runtime-identity probe. Python build/platform, installation prefixes, standard library and Meson module locations/version must match. Distinct binaries, copies from another directory, lookup errors and differing runtime/module identities remain failures. No installed files, aliases, versions or completed output stages are changed.

Twenty-four shell fixtures now pass, including six new alias/mismatch/error regressions using actual hardlinks/copies and real Make/pkg-config. The corrected production helper also passes a real NDK r26d compile/archive check with Docker networking disabled, paths containing spaces and a normal VPS user. The unchanged PowerShell wrapper retains its previous ten passing fixtures; Bash syntax and pasted PowerShell command parsing also pass. ## Windows LLVM archive-pipe failure and configure correction (2026-10-12)

Attachment 3b83fdf6-006c-4d48-af27-b270417c9f29 contains 114 repeated LLVM output-stream errors. The prior text-base receipt, all three cached source hashes, selected host tools and NDK version were accepted. gettext-runtime configure completed compiler/linker/headers/ELF checks and printed library suffixes lib,lib,lib, then the repeated errors began. The log ends with the helper stopping its owned process tree and three successful taskkill termination lines. It contains no GLib dependency build success, artifact receipt success or ADB test. Preceding Git pulls and exact Windows HEADs are not shown.

The next native operation in the pinned release configure is `${AR-ar} -p "$dir/$libname.$acl_libext" | acl_is_expected_elfclass`, first checking the verified libiconv.a. Its POSIX reader uses `od -A n -t d1 -j 4 -N 1`, which reads only the ELF-class byte and closes input while llvm-ar still writes the object stream. The [primary LLVM bug report](https://www.mail-archive.com/llvm-bugs@lists.llvm.org/msg49040.html) records this Windows failure when a consumer exits early: a closed-pipe WriteFile error becomes EINVAL and LLVM reports an output-stream failure. The exact source position, returned log and independent pipe reproduction strongly support this diagnosis; no Windows process/handle trace was collected, and the exact fatal-message loop was not reproduced on Linux. This differs from the previously corrected invalid-stdin failure and occurs before parallel Make.

The new plan applies two exact checked edits only to the extracted gettext-runtime/configure. Both the POSIX od reader and BSD dd/hexdump fallback consume the remaining input with cat after extracting the class. The class result and read/drain status remain available; the selected Bash fallback confines pipefail to a subshell so dd failures are preserved. Archive format selection remains active. Cached source archives, original engine files, NDK, previous helpers, completed text-base inputs, generated tool wrappers, compiler options and runtime probe are unchanged.

The new configure bytes intentionally change the managed source marker. NEXT_TEST.md preserves the previous build/android-glib-deps as a unique timestamp/GUID sibling before creating a fresh owned stage. There is no marker bypass or automatic overwrite. The VPS previous complete stage was preserved at build/android-glib-deps-before-pipe-fix-20261012T201111Z; its receipt hash and the prior text-base receipt hash are recorded in build/android-glib-deps-pipe-fix-before.json. GIRepository headers and metadata and unbuilt utility/code-generator variables are excluded. GLib's two exact internal COPYING aliases are materialized from their regular LGPL license target for Windows portability; unexpected links, unsafe/colliding paths and altered source/prefix bytes remain rejected. The probe checks exact GLib2.82.1 compile/runtime versions, Unicode conversion with invalid-input rejection, Unicode regex named captures, serialized variant data and wrong-type rejection, generic libffi-backed GObject signals and destruction, GIO gzip streams and closed-stream errors, worker-thread dispatch to a private main context, and GModule process-symbol lookup. It uses no network or assets. ## Expat, Fontconfig and Cairo rendering stage prepared (2026-10-12)

The separate build/android-text-render stage uses the existing cache's Expat2.5.0, Fontconfig2.17.1 and Cairo1.16.0 with NDK26.3.11579264/x86_64/API23. The new driver binds 15 project inputs: the union of unchanged font/text-base inputs plus its own driver/plan/probe. Both prerequisite receipts and complete installed inventories are verified and recorded. The older fonts builder has no portable verify-only API, so a new read-only adapter validates its existing receipt, metadata, Android ELF/static libraries and full prefix without requiring an NDK or modifying that helper. The completed GLib/media/SDL and other stages remain intact.

Expat and Cairo build only their release configure/Make library targets. Fontconfig uses Meson with its Expat XML backend, verified FreeType and libiconv, explicitly empty default/additional font directories, and documentation/tests/tools/cache generation/NLS/fontations disabled. Cairo enables image, FreeType and Fontconfig while disabling PNG, vector, desktop/window-system, OpenGL, GObject, interpreter and tracing facilities. Source inspection of restored engine text paths found no use of the disabled Cairo PNG/vector/GObject helpers; PangoCairo later uses plain cairo/cairo-ft. Fontconfig metadata adds a private iconv package dependency so static consumers receive the earlier include and library paths. Only the three selected archives, public headers, five portable pkg-config files, licenses and one C probe are installed. The new executable uses no HarfBuzz/C++ calls; the old font prefix's shared C++ runtime is verified as part of that prefix but is not transferred for this probe.

The probe validates pinned library versions and five behaviors. Expat parses namespace-aware UTF-8 XML in two chunks, decodes entities, rejects mismatched tags and invokes an external-entity rejection handler without external I/O. A private FcConfig is parsed from memory and registers only the two pinned font files; it selects Lato for Latin and uses charset matching to fall back to DejaVu Sans for Arabic U+0639, which Lato lacks. FcConfigSetDefaultSubstitute explicitly uses this private configuration; the compatibility FcDefaultSubstitute would pass NULL and permit global configuration. Cairo receives resolved FC_FILE patterns, avoiding its global Fontconfig lookup fallback. Image tests verify premultiplied alpha, OVER compositing and clipping; glyph tests verify nonzero glyphs/extents, changed pixels, opaque output and untouched corners for Latin text and an Arabic character. Full paragraph shaping/bidirectional analysis is not tested.

The device helper uses the real native-Python receipt verifier, then validates the exact two TTF files and COPYING notice against the unchanged native-fonts/assets.json before any ADB action. It rejects redirected/missing/changed assets, uses the booted primary-x86_64/API23-or-later selected device and pushes only the executable plus those three files. No shared C++ library, APK, full dataset copy, server process or on-screen interaction is needed. Complete console output remains the requested Windows result.

Independent normal-user, network-disabled NDK prototypes built the three static libraries, but the first combined Android probe link exposed an incorrect Fontconfig Meson setting. Detecting external GNU libiconv caused Fontconfig2.17.1 to define LIBICONV_PLUG. That suppresses the external iconv.h public-name mappings and requests system iconv symbols unavailable at Android API23. The new plan replaces exactly that one definition with an explanatory comment in the newly extracted meson.build. It requires exactly one original anchor and records the corrected source inventory; cached sources and all prior prefixes remain unchanged. The incomplete owned VPS stage was retained under a unique android-text-render-before-iconv-* name before rebuilding. No Windows backup is needed for this previously undelivered stage.

A real NDK regression reproduces the old iconv_open/iconv/iconv_close link failure and confirms that the checked correction selects libiconv_open/libiconv/libiconv_close from the completed GNU1.17 archive. An independent compilation of actual Fontconfig fcfreetype.c confirms the same symbol change. The first host semantic check had been masked by host libc providing system iconv; its result alone did not prove GNU libiconv use. Fontconfig was rebuilt with the correction, and the host link map now proves members from all six intended archives, including GNU libiconv. A generated HOST_ONLY copy of the latest probe passes every semantic group with FONTCONFIG_FILE, FONTCONFIG_PATH and FONTCONFIG_SYSROOT pointing to nonexistent locations, without global Fontconfig warnings. Four negative cases (missing font, swapped font arguments, wrong alpha expectation and wrong Expat error expectation) each exit1 at the intended check without aggregate success. This establishes host semantics, not Android runtime. ## Windows Cairo temporary-file failure and scoped recovery (2026-10-12)

Attachment7ac225f1-bf94-44a3-bf15-39c68a3d46bf shows the existing MSYS Python3.12.13/Make4.4.1/Meson1.12.0/Ninja1.13.2/pkg-config3.0.5/NDK26.3.11579264 toolchain verified both prerequisite prefixes, built Expat and all37 Fontconfig steps, then failed during Cairo configure. The reported lines37058 and38535 start large here-documents appended to config.status. Bash printed cannot create temp file for here-document: Device or resource busy twice, followed by configure: error: write failure creating ./config.status. The builder exited2; Cairo compilation, the final probe and device helper were not reached. No exact Windows HEAD is shown in the excerpt.

The Cairo source connects those errors to config.status generation. GNU Bash5.3 redir.c and lib/sh/tmpfile.c show that the generic here-document error can report temporary-file creation, writing, reopening or unlinking failure; the message alone does not identify the failed syscall. TMPDIR controls the shell temporary directory. MSYS error mapping can map several Windows statuses to EBUSY, so neither the current process owner nor the historical Git-blocking service is established by this log. Do not change config.status ACLs, system services, antivirus settings or installed tool versions from this evidence. Primary source: https://ftp.gnu.org/gnu/bash/bash-5.3.tar.gz; audit files remain under build/audit-cairo-heredoc-20261013.

The driver now gives only Cairo configure a unique private TMPDIR under build/cairo-configure-attempts/attempt-*/tmp. Each attempt tees complete combined stdout/stderr to configure.log while preserving readable EOF stdin. A nonzero exit triggers another complete configure run only when both the exact Bash busy-here-document diagnostic and the config.status write-failure line appeared. There are at most three total attempts, with1s/2s delays; ordinary compiler/configure failures stop immediately. Failure logs, temporary files and guarded copies of config.log/config.status are retained. Compilation starts only after configure succeeds. The caller environment and every previous helper remain unchanged.

No source, build-plan, generated-toolchain or ownership-marker format changes were needed. Existing owned Windows stage inputs therefore still match; the partial Expat/Fontconfig prefix remains guarded and reusable. Preserve the current build/android-text-render directory and rerun the same helpers after normal pulls. Do not rename/delete the stage or edit files manually. This is a targeted recovery for possible transient temporary-file conflicts, not proof of the underlying Windows cause or of a successful Windows fix.

Independent Linux Bash5.2.21 tracing used the exact76,160-byte quoted Cairo here-document. Its create/reopen/unlink operations stayed inside the supplied private TMPDIR. An isolated test shim injected one EBUSY at unlink and reproduced the exact Bash error, demonstrating why the error wording does not prove creation was the failed operation. The unchanged new runner retained attempt1 failure evidence, waited1s and completed attempt2 in a distinct directory; config.status matched the expected bytes. Caller environment, source and prior prefix files remained unchanged. This is an injected Linux regression, not a native Windows reproduction. Evidence: build/audit-cairo-heredoc-20261013/{real-heredoc-check.log,real-heredoc-result.json,real-runner-check.log,real-runner-result.json}.

All24 focused builder tests pass without skips in the network-disabled verification image. New cases cover exact two-diagnostic retry filtering, one-failure recovery, three-attempt exhaustion, immediate failure on unrelated errors, EOF stdin, distinct temporary paths, retained combined output/config snapshots, parent environment and existing file preservation, redirected-path rejection, and real oversized quoted/expanding Bash here-documents under paths containing spaces. Evidence: build/android-text-render-cairo-retry-tests.log.

The actual existing-stage Android rebuild completed with networking disabled and the normal VPS user. Cairo configure succeeded on its first private-directory attempt, libraries/probe linked, all inventories passed and the updated receipt verified. Every earlier font/text-base/GLib-prerequisite/GLib prefix and receipt is byte-identical. ## FriBidi source preparation for offline paragraph layout (2026-10-13)

The user requested the next step after the confirmed TextRender result. This delivery adds one separately pinned source archive and a Windows PowerShell 5.1-compatible preparation helper. It prepares a dependency; it does not build Pango, replace completed native libraries, create an APK or execute an Android test. The existing source cache contains Pango 1.50.14 but lacks FriBidi. Pango requires FriBidi >=1.0.6; its bundled unpinned master fallback must remain disabled.

The selected [official FriBidi 1.0.17 release](https://github.com/fribidi/fribidi/releases/tag/v1.0.17) was published 2026-09-20. The [official release metadata](https://api.github.com/repos/fribidi/fribidi/releases/tags/v1.0.17) and independent download agree: fribidi-1.0.17.tar.xz, 1,203,284 bytes, SHA256 6949dcde27d41cebad1fd741fcafc36d55a1020d2d872d4a6eb3914caabbada2. The archive contains 189 regular files/20,305,881 bytes, with no links or casefold collisions. Its source version is 1.0.17; the release tag points to commit b93119f5fdc7ea47672cc304c1455ffa6dfe7536. GitHub reports the tag signature as verified; no local GPG verification is claimed. LGPL-2.1-or-later and the bundled Unicode notices must remain with future distribution.

The new manifest is tools/native-text-layout/sources.json. Prepare-WindowsAndroidLayoutSources.ps1 uses only Windows PowerShell/.NET facilities and takes one optional -Download switch. Default mode verifies an existing regular archive at build/android-layout-sources/fribidi-1.0.17.tar.xz without creating directories or making a network request. Missing input exits2 with the connected preparation instruction. A mismatched existing archive is preserved and rejected. Download mode reuses a matching archive without networking; otherwise it streams the exact official HTTPS release into a unique CreateNew temporary file, checks response size, total bytes and SHA256, then uses a non-overwriting rename. Request and read timeouts are 60s. Failed/interrupted downloads clean only their owned temporary file. Links/reparse points and unexpected response destinations are rejected; the process TLS setting is restored. The original 23-source manifest and completed build stages are unchanged.

Independent source/build audit found a release-specific constraint for the later native builder: the archive includes stale Unicode 14 generated files in lib/ while bundled UCD inputs and Meson generation use 18.0.0. Quoted includes prefer source/lib, so an untouched Meson build silently uses stale tables even though it regenerates new ones. Clang include tracing and original/corrected build contents prove this. Preserve the untouched archive, but the future owned extraction must verify and omit exactly these seven generated files; keep hand-authored lib/arabic-misc.tab.i. This helper only downloads/verifies; it does not extract or alter them.

Attachmentb83b39ee-d59b-437d-a6de-d04f1446eabc is 183,213 bytes/SHA256 bd65fbc6c6e063e627bd5f3b8d4f824b5ec67859a9faca72833b7516b056943c, saved on Windows as diagnostics/network-20261013-065142-325.txt. Existing OpenSSL generation is verified and reused. Native CMake4.3/Ninja1.13.2/NDKr26d Clang17 build with -- -d keepdepfile reaches1034/1034 steps, links libcrypto.a and libssl.a, and installs them. The second configure finds OpenSSL3.4.1 in the owned prefix. Thus both previous header and depfile corrections now pass on Windows. No pulled commit IDs appear. Earlier completed stages remain preserved; no package changes are indicated.

Curl configuration completes in722.2seconds and generation in3.8seconds, with HTTP/HTTPS and OpenSSL selected. CMake then invokes Ninja -C the existing build directory -t restat for build.ninja and three cmake_install.cmake manifests. Ninja reports `failed recompaction: Permission denied`, causing CMake generation to fail. Curl compilation, final Network receipt and Android execution are not reached. The numerous configure feature-test failures are platform probes, not the terminating error. The holding process and precise failed file operation are unknown.

[Ninja1.13.2 BuildLog::Restat](https://github.com/ninja-build/ninja/blob/v1.13.2/src/build_log.cc#L362-L413) writes .ninja_log.restat, closes it, unlinks the original .ninja_log, then renames the temporary. A failure may therefore leave the original present or absent. [ToolRestat](https://github.com/ninja-build/ninja/blob/v1.13.2/src/ninja.cc#L1038-L1090) labels this as failed recompaction and can treat a missing original as nothing to do; a blind configure retry risks losing incremental history and rebuilding completed objects. [CMake4.3 CleanMetaData](https://github.com/Kitware/CMake/blob/v4.3.0/Source/cmGlobalNinjaGenerator.cxx#L626-L690) makes this post-generation call; CMAKE_SUPPRESS_REGENERATION does not selectively disable it. No speculative build retry, metadata repair or tool-version substitution is delivered.

The safe next step is tools/inspect_android_network_state.py, invoked by current NEXT_TEST.md with the existing native Python. It reads fixed .ninja_log/.ninja_log.restat/.ninja_log.recompact, preserving exact contents in a JSON report alongside sizes/hashes, v7-format/row counts, and final-newline information. .ninja_deps and build.ninja are fingerprinted without reporting their contents. Saved generation and the partial prefix are checked with existing read-only validators, without requiring a final Network receipt or running native tools. A syntactically valid temporary log and plausible record count do not prove completeness or authorize recovery. ## Native Alliance account connection component (2026-10-20)

New client alliance_native/transport.hpp/.cpp and tools/native-alliance/CMakeLists.txt/probe.cpp implement a separate C++17 worker-thread transport. The pinned engine/source manifests, existing guest connection/fallback, APK/OBB and all earlier native stages are unchanged. This is not yet selected by game UI. Trusted profile JSON import and account semantics are separate remaining work; the component accepts typed public fields and independently validates canonical unicast IP, port, one bounded leaf certificate, validity/server purpose/IP identity and exact SHA256 DER pin. It uses a private OpenSSL library context/trust store, TLS minimum1.2, native preamble1, exact zero acceptance, and no downgrade/retry/redirect path. Sends require verified connection state.

Connect/send/receive are separate operations for server-push support, with no operation queue,64KiB compressed-frame bound, monotonic deadlines and atomic cancellation polled within20ms. Timeouts close the socket, and each operation accepts at most10seconds. This is a worker-only bounded primitive; a future UI adapter must handle scheduling, normal idle waits/reconnection and bounded WML decompression/schema validation. It is not a concurrent full game network loop and does not replace large game/scenario transfers.

New server Test-WindowsAllianceNative.ps1 and test_alliance_native.py build only the two new native source files into a unique build/alliance-native-<runid>, read the existing public profile, then generate disposable identities in a new restricted fixture directory. The original private identity/MongoDB runtime are not read or written. All sockets are loopback/temporary ports; the real Ethernet100.99.99.14 is validated offline as identity, not contacted. Selected Windows pins are checked before build and compiled Boost/OpenSSL versions are checked again. New text/JSON diagnostics retain partial failures and safe error codes. No dependency downloads, service starts, .env usage or game reinstallation.

## Stale launcher owner confirmed and OS guard introduced (2026-10-20)

Returned `runtime-lock-20261020-070719-282-2357380e3baf49338965cdd36cc46699.txt`:1,288bytes/SHA25601627245b5ce882debbfbf5a3f439858c162769bbe1849f506f8fb1771c77b87. At07:07:19.3093669-06:00 it records profile operation.lock,63bytes, LastWriteTimeUtc12:36:21, pid7400/started_at2026-10-20T12:36:21.894725+00:00; recorded owner no longer running, no matching Python/MongoDB processes, and no listeners27018/18765. The prior log's MongoDB PID1880 had completed storage shutdown. This establishes a stale marker at observation time, not indefinite liveness or application-record persistence. No area/audit/logout result has yet been supplied.

The previous launcher marker relied on Python finally to unlink it; interruption could bypass that cleanup. New alliance/runtime_lock.py holds a permanent non-inherited operation.guard handle with Windows msvcrt nonblocking byte-range locking (offset0,length1), or POSIX flock for Linux. The guard is never unlinked; process termination releases its OS lock. Initialization and database ownership still hold exclusion through cleanup. Missing/regular/single-link/path-identity/content checks are conservative; the guard is zero/one NUL byte, initialized only while held. Legacy operation.lock is refused using lstat so inaccessible metadata is not mistaken for absence. Port checks and MongoDB storage locking still protect against orphan database processes. A lock failure is never permission to delete the guard. No database schema/credentials/settings or Python/MongoDB/package versions change.

Repair-WindowsAllianceRuntimeLock.ps1 performs a separate one-time legacy recovery after the code update. It requires explicit runtime, accepts expectedPID/time together, verifies owned/path/handle/link/size/JSON identities, retains read/delete access to the exact marker with writes denied, acquires the same Windows guard byte lock, and requires two successful current owner/Python/MongoDB/port checks. It preserves the checked marker by SetFileInformationByHandle(FileRenameInfo) to a unique same-directory backup without overwrite. It prints only safe metadata and saves a transcript. No database connection, process termination, DBlock deletion or private credential read occurs. Updated-only launchers and quiescent migration are required; old code does not recognize operation.guard.

Windows APIs cannot be executed by the VPS tests. Primary semantics reviewed: Python3.14 msvcrt.locking permits ranges beyond EOF and LK_NBLCK fails immediately (https://docs.python.org/3.14/library/msvcrt.html); Windows file locks release on handle close/process termination, possibly after a brief delay (https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/fileapi/nf-fileapi-lockfileex); Microsoft .NET Framework FileStream.Lock maps to Win32 LockFile (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/microsoft/referencesource/main/mscorlib/system/io/filestream.cs). Actual Windows Python/.NET guard/recovery/stop-restart acceptance remains pending.

Final VPS verification:12 focused guard checks pass under pinned Python3.14.3, including real competing processes, normal/forced exit with the same permanent file, legacy refusal, descriptor lifetime, metadata errors and mocked Windows byte-range calls. All15 deployment checks pass in the existing network-disabled Python3.14.3/MongoDB8.0.32 container, including authenticated initialization/restart, scoped access, service startup, rollback and occupied-port refusal. The recovery helper passes16 tests/45 PowerShell invocations plus a separate live PowerShell byte-lock holder; native C# compilation succeeds. Windows handles/renaming are shimmed for Linux execution, so this is not actual Windows API acceptance. Independent source review passes; four exact handoff blocks parse, five shared documents match, and233 of234 snapshotted existing implementation/helper/test/README files remain identical (deployment.py is the intended changed existing source). No original runtime, secret, binary, cache or data was accessed by the test container. The current code also checks CPython3.14.3 stat/fstat file identity behavior against upstream source: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/python/cpython/v3.14.3/Python/fileutils.c . Evidence: server build/alliance-audit/runtime-lock-fix-20261020/verification.json and container logs. Windows recovery/stop-restart remains the next required result.

## Standalone Android Alliance transport delivery (2026-10-20)

New client tools/build_android_alliance.py, Build-WindowsAndroidAlliance.ps1 and Test-AndroidAlliance.ps1 build and execute a small console fixture, not a new APK. Existing native-alliance CMake adds a default-OFF WESNOTH_ALLIANCE_ANDROID_CHECK option; AndroidCheck.cmake adds only the android-alliance-check target. The production alliance_native transport and original native probe are unchanged. Build uses the verified existing Boost1.88 headers, OpenSSL3.4.1 archives and identical shared C++ runtime; no dependencies, engine or OBB are rebuilt. Owned build/android-alliance contains two installed files. Source/recipe/prerequisite receipt binding covers32 project inputs. It refuses changed/interrupted configure metadata, configures once with automatic regeneration suppressed, retains Ninja depfiles, and verifies a completed receipt without any compiler call. Build-operation interruption is preserved for diagnosis, not automatically recovered. The small foreground Windows build has no wall-clock timeout; device verifier/ADB calls are bounded.

The new C++ fixture uses ephemeral in-memory EC certificates and peers bound only to127.0.0.1. It exercises the actual Client in22 cases: before-connect guards, wrong IP/pin, hostname/broadcast refusal, expiry, TLS1.2/TLS1.3 exact gzip-canary round trips, server push, untrusted peer, downgrade/plaintext, preamble EOF/reset/stall, TLS stall, oversized/empty/partial frames, receive stall and cancellation. Exact error and stage must match. Timer tests additionally check elapsed bounds below the peer's7-second emergency deadline, preventing that deadline from falsely proving client timeout/cancellation. All peer I/O is bounded and joined. This fixture does not independently count reconnect attempts or early application bytes; do not broaden its claims to replace the separate earlier Windows fixture. No production identity/key, database, APK, game data or external endpoint is used.

APK 1192605 implements Transfer captain, Leave team and Area rankings. Transfer requires the current captain and another current member; actor, target and team must remain eligible/idle. The former captain remains a member. Ordinary members may leave; a captain with other members must transfer first. A sole captain sees the explicit archive warning, including cleared pending applications and removal from active lists/rankings. The exact native leave body is {team_id, allow_archive:boolean}. The request defaults false; the UI sets true only after the archive warning's affirmative action. Expected membership and archive consent are checked inside every MongoDB transaction attempt. A stale ordinary leave cannot archive a team after another member leaves/transfers command. Client and bridge reject archived:true if consent was false. Legacy domain leave behavior remains unchanged.

Rankings query at most 21 active teams and return at most 20 public summaries, ordered by rating descending then UUID ascending, with a strict has_more boolean. Requested area, response order, uniqueness, numeric limits and field sets are checked by bridge and client. More teams remain available through the existing paged directory. Transfer responses bind the requested team and selected target; leave responses bind the team and authenticated actor, with the game checking the latter against its captured player profile. Unknown/extra fields and malformed/ambiguous responses fail closed. The new directory transfer wrapper maps a suspended selected target to forbidden/HTTP403, preserving the caller's valid session; the legacy domain API still reports suspended. An actual401 invalidates the caller's session.

Approval/rejection choice and confirmation menus, transfer selection/confirmation and leave confirmation begin on Back without changes. Opening that default row, Back or Escape performs no mutation. Create/apply/withdraw retain their established confirmations. Successful management acknowledgements trigger a separate My team read; a later read failure does not erase the acknowledged result. Cancelled/timed-out/discarded/malformed acknowledgements show uncertainty and direct the player to My team; no mutation is automatically retried. Personal/team XP, rating and roster capacity rules remain unchanged.

The user explicitly requests password reset and an Edit feature on the existing admin website. Server implementation0326a77 adds Edit and Reset password links to the account list/detail. Edit supports unique ASCII username and Active/Suspended status. Reset is separate and requires exact matching12–128-character Unicode passwords and an audit reason. Account ID, role, personal leader/XP, team/member references, application history, battle plans and reservations remain intact. Passwords are hashed with the existing Argon2 settings; neither submitted password field is returned in HTML, cookies, URLs or generated audit data. Password errors leave both controls blank. Audit reasons are arbitrary operator-entered text with a warning not to include credentials.

Reset updates the hash, revokes every target session, clears only that role/name's current and previous15-minute attempt buckets and records reset_password atomically. A reset can recover access during an active run without releasing that run; suspension remains unchanged. Same-password reset is allowed and still ends sessions. Username/status edits retain the existing idle-account requirement, end target sessions on actual changes, and record old/new username/status in edit_account. Renames clear scoped old/new-name attempt buckets. An unchanged save records an audit/revision but does not revoke sessions. Own-name changes and own-password resets sign the administrator out. Self-suspension and removal of the last active administrator are rejected.

Both new forms require CSRF/admin/loopback authorization, exact single-valued fields, a reason and the submitted account revision. Concurrent changes produce a stale form requiring explicit reload; stale/double submissions do not repeat mutations. Authorizing administrators are rechecked transactionally and self-edits consume one revision. Authentication now rechecks username_key alongside the original hash/status/role before issuing a session, preventing an in-flight old-name login from surviving a rename. The existing legacy status POST remains compatible; the new UI uses the revision-protected editor. No schema/index migration, dependency, native protocol, bridge, Android or game-data change is needed.
